<meta>This page continues the literary catalog of Book II. Following the mentions of Homer and Hesiod on the previous page, the author now acknowledges poets of pastoral, didactic, and underworld themes before pivotally asserting his own originality. He transitions from "well-trodden" poetic paths to the "untouched springs" of his astrological subject matter—the divine spirit that animates the universe.</meta>

He sings of the sweet countryside and leads the Muse into the breezes <note>original: *rura sonat*; likely a reference to Theocritus or the tradition of pastoral poetry.</note>.
Behold, another tells of painted birds and the wars of wild beasts.
That one recounts venomous snakes; this one the fates born through herbs, 
And those plants bearing life from their own roots <note>This likely refers to Nicander of Colophon, a Greek poet who wrote the *Theriaca* (about venomous creatures) and *Alexipharmaca* (about poisons and their herbal antidotes).</note>.
Furthermore, they even call the Tartarus <note>The deepest abyss of the underworld in Greek mythology.</note> of immense darkness 
Into the light; they unroll the inner world from the night,
Having broken the treaty of nature's custom.
The learned sisters <note>The Muses, the nine daughters of Zeus and Mnemosyne who preside over the arts and sciences.</note> have sung of every kind of thing;
Every path to the approaches of Helicon <note>Mount Helicon, the sacred home of the Muses; Manilius is saying that common poetic themes are overused.</note> is now well-trodden.
And now the rivers flow muddy from the springs,
Nor can they provide a fresh draught for the crowd rushing to the sacred rites.
Let us seek out untouched meadows through dewy herbs,
And a wave meditating its murmur in hidden caves,
Which neither birds have tasted with hard beaks,
Nor Phoebus <note>The sun god, Apollo, often associated with poetic inspiration.</note> himself has sipped, except with ethereal fire.
I shall speak my own things: I shall owe no utterance to any of the bards,
Nor will this work come as a theft; I fly alone 
In my chariot into the sky; I drive the waves in my own boat.
I shall sing of her <note>Nature personified.</note>, powerful with a silent mind,
And of the God infused into the lands, the sky, and the sea,
Governing the immense mass with an equal law,
And the whole world living by an alternating consensus,
And driven by a living motion; since one spirit <term>Spiritus Mundi: The "World Soul" or "Universal Spirit," a central concept in Stoic philosophy suggesting the universe is a living, breathing organism permeated by a divine rational force.</term>
Dwells through all parts and waters the world,
Flying through all things and shaping the world into the body of a living creature.

<summary>Manilius dismisses overused poetic themes like pastoral and underworld myths to claim a unique path for his work. He introduces the Stoic doctrine of the Spiritus Mundi, the divine soul that animates and unifies the entire cosmos.</summary>
<keywords>literary tradition, Muses, Helicon, poetic originality, Spiritus Mundi, Stoicism, Nature, cosmology</keywords>

--- Page 51 ---
<meta>This page continues Manilius’s philosophical justification for his work. After claiming his poetic independence on the previous page, he now argues that the universe is not a collection of random atoms (opposing the Epicurean view), but a deliberate "machine" governed by a divine mind or Reason. He uses the regularity of nature—tides, seasons, and planetary orbits—as proof of this celestial governance.</meta>

And unless this world-machine <note>original: *machina*; Manilius views the universe as a complex, functional apparatus rather than a chaotic void.</note> remained woven together by related parts and the whole obeyed an appointed master, and unless a divine providence ruled over such a vast cosmic consciousness, there would be no stability for the earth, and no orbital paths for the stars. The world would falter in its wandering and grow stiff by standing still, nor would the ages preserve their appointed courses. Nor would alternating night flee the day and in turn put it to flight; the winds would not nourish shadows for the earth nor the upper air; the sea would not nourish heavy clouds, nor the rivers the sea, nor the ocean its springs. The whole would not remain constant through all its parts, which are always organized in a balanced way by a creator, so that neither the waters nor the stars would fail us, and the sky would revolve neither faster nor slower than is right.

Motion sustains the work, it does not change it; thus all things remain distributed throughout the whole world and follow their lord. This God, therefore, and the Reason <note>original: *ratio*; in Stoic philosophy, the *Logos* or divine logic that structures the universe.</note> which governs all things, guides earthly creatures by means of the eternal signs <note>The constellations of the Zodiac.</note>. Although these signs are removed from us by a vast distance, the Reason causes them to be felt so that it may provide life and destiny to the nations, and instill specific characters into individual bodies.

Nor is evidence for this far to seek: thus the sky tempers the fields, thus it yields and snatches away the various fruits of the earth; thus it moves the sea, casting it upon the land and drawing it back. And as for this upheaval of the sea: now the movements of the moon <note>original: *luna mota*; Manilius correctly identifies the moon as the primary influence on the tides.</note> control the waters, now they are stimulated by its alternating retreat...

<summary>Manilius argues that the perfect order of the cosmos—the cycles of day and night, seasons, and tides—proves the existence of a divine Reason (Ratio) that governs both the stars and human destiny.</summary>
<keywords>Stoicism, Ratio, Divine Providence, Machina Mundi, Tides, Zodiacal influence, Cosmology</keywords>

--- Page 52 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous folio, where Manilius asserted that a divine Reason (Ratio) governs the universe. Having established that the macrocosm of the stars and the microcosm of Earth are linked by a single logic, he now provides specific examples from nature—ranging from the behavior of shellfish to the instincts of cattle—to prove that all life is attuned to the celestial clockwork. This leads to his famous conclusion regarding the divine nature of the human mind.</meta>

Now as the Sun <note>original: *Phoebus*; a common poetic name for the sun god.</note> traverses the broad space, flying on,
so even creatures submerged in the waves and enclosed in the prison of their shells
vary their bodies according to the movement of the moon.
And when your losses, Moon <note>original: *Delia*; an epithet for the goddess Diana/the Moon, born on the island of Delos.</note>, imitate your strengths <note>A reference to the moon’s phases—as it loses its light (losses), it prepares to regain it (strengths).</note>,
you likewise return the light to your nearby horns as it rises,
and again you seek back from those stars whatever the Sun leaves behind,
or you bring back what he gave; thus star is balanced by star.
Finally, even the cattle and the mute animals of the earth,
though they remain ignorant of themselves and the laws of the gods <note>original: *superum*; refers to the "ones above."</note>,
yet as Nature, their parent, calls them back to the universe,
they lift their spirits and observe the heavens and the stars.
They purify their bodies toward the horns of the new moon,
and they foresee coming winters and the return of clear skies.
Who could doubt after this that man is joined to the heavens?
For Nature gave him an exceptional tongue, a capable
intellect, and a swift soul—into which, at last,
God descends and dwells, seeking himself within.
He mixed in other arts to which a will is granted,
so faithful to us, though they are not of our own making <note>original: *census*; literally "wealth" or "inventory," here meaning our inherent earthly nature or "account."</note>.
He mixed them equally, and nothing is granted except under law,
as the creators of the human body <note>The stars or planetary deities responsible for physical formation in astrological theory.</note> would allow.
He also mixed in invincible and inevitable fate;
it is the lot of matter to be driven, but the lot of the universe to drive.
Who could know the heavens except by the gifts of the heavens?
And who could find God, unless he who is himself a part of the gods?

<summary>Manilius argues that all life, from shellfish to cattle, instinctively responds to celestial cycles, proving that the human mind—which shares in the divine essence—is uniquely capable of understanding the heavens and the laws of Fate.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, Stoicism, lunar cycles, animal instinct, divine spark, Fate, Manilius, microcosm, macrocosm</keywords>

--- Page 53 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical argument from folio 21v. Having argued that animals possess an instinctive connection to the heavens, Manilius now pivots to the human mind. He asserts that our ability to comprehend the vast, complex structures of the universe is not accidental, but proof that the human spirit is a "kindred mind" to the divine creator.</meta>

And [who could doubt that man could] perceive this interconnected mass of an infinite Creator <note>original: *parentis*; literally "parent," referring here to the divine architect of the universe.</note>, the dances of the constellations, and the flaming vaults of the world; the eternal war of stars against stars, the lands, the sea beneath the sky, and all things subject to both, enclosing them within the small space of his heart?

Unless Nature had given such great eyes to our spirits, and turned a mind akin to herself toward herself, and commanded that so great a work should come from the heavens? This mind calls us to the heavens, to a sacred communion <note>original: *commercia*; implies an exchange or relationship between the human and the divine.</note> with the universe, and to the primary laws that the stars grant to those at their birth. Who would deny that it were a sacrilege to seize the universe against its will and lead it, like a captive, into the city of oneself?

If these truths are presented clearly without long-winded detours, the sheer reliability of the evidence will grant weight and credibility to the work. For Reason <note>original: *ratio*; the governing logic of the universe and the human faculty of understanding.</note> is neither deceived, nor does it ever deceive. The path must be carved correctly and entrusted to true causes, so that the outcome is granted exactly as it was foreseen. Let Fortune confirm this; who would dare to call it false and overrule the vast testimony of Fate?

I desire to bear these stars aloft with a divine inspiration <note>original: *flatu*; literally "breath" or "gust," implying poetic or prophetic inspiration.</note>, and I shall not compose these songs for the common crowd, nor bury them within it. I am as one driving a chariot alone in an empty world, where no others block the path, traveling a road where none share my journey, singing of things the heavens must know while the stars themselves look on in wonder.

<summary>Manilius argues that the human mind's ability to grasp the complexity of the cosmos proves its divine origin and kinship with the stars. He positions himself as a solitary poet-prophet, guided by Reason to reveal the laws of Fate to an audience beyond the "common crowd."</summary>

<keywords>Manilius, Astronomica, cosmology, Nature, divinity of the mind, Reason (Ratio), Fate, poetic inspiration, microcosm</keywords>

--- Page 54 ---
<meta>This page continues the transition from Manilius’s philosophical prologue into the technical foundations of astrology. Following the previous page's argument that the human mind is uniquely suited to understand the heavens, the poet now identifies who is worthy of this knowledge and begins to categorize the signs of the zodiac by gender and form.</meta>

While the world rejoices in its own nature through the songs of poets <note>original: *uates*; a term implying both a poet and a divinely inspired prophet.</note>, or those to whom the world has not begrudged its sacred paths and knowledge of itself—it is the smallest crowd throughout the entire world. That other crowd is the one that loves riches and gold, the impious pursuit of power <note>original: *fasces*; the bundles of rods and axes carried before Roman magistrates, symbolizing political authority.</note>, and soft luxury found in idleness; they are enticed by flattering sounds and sweet pleasures that affect the ears, yet the fates are meant to be understood with only a modest amount of labor. It is also a gift of the fates to learn the very law of fate.

And first, the varied nature of the stars must be noted in my songs, according to each gender: for there are six masculine signs, and just as many of a different kind <note>The "different kind" refers to feminine signs.</note>, starting with the Bull <term>Taurus: considered the first feminine sign in this sequence</term> as the leader. You can see how, as they return and rise into an arc, they face one another; they alternate their gender and vary by turns around the circle. You will see human shapes in some parts, yet the character of cattle and wild beasts is not far off in others. They will create the natural disposition of the soul, which must now be marked by a keen mind, as each sign is carried along by its own lot.

### Which signs are double and of two-forms

Now pay attention to the double signs; these pairings will grant powerful effects through their Lot <term>Lot: the *Sors*, a specific calculation in ancient astrology determining a person's destiny</term>. A companion adds much and takes much away, and these fellowships of fate are powerful in their ambiguity, leading toward both merit and harm. Behold the two Fishes <note>Pisces</note> among the stars, and just as many Twins <note>Gemini</note> with their limbs bared. Their arms remain joined in a bond with another, yet their gaze is different, as if looking in opposite directions.

<summary>Manilius distinguishes those seeking spiritual truth from those seeking worldly power before detailing the genders of the zodiac signs and the nature of "double" signs like Gemini and Pisces.</summary>
<keywords>zodiac genders, masculine and feminine signs, Taurus, Gemini, Pisces, Stoic fate, astrological character, double signs</keywords>

--- Page 55 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of the zodiac signs. Having previously categorized the signs by gender (masculine and feminine) and their general orientation in the sky, Manilius now moves to the "double" (bicorpora) and "two-formed" (bi-form) signs, explaining how their physical appearance reflects the transition between the seasons.</meta>

But a different nature must be noted for those that are paired. From these pairs, there are signs that remain throughout the whole sky rejoicing in themselves, changed in nothing externally; yet others seem to grieve for what is lost, as if a certain part had been cut away, and from a divided body, limbs have been joined together. 

For instance, consider the Archer <note>original: *capricornus*; the scribe has likely substituted Capricorn here, but the description "who directs the drawn bow" (*qui tentum dirigit arcum*) clearly refers to Sagittarius.</note> who directs a drawn bow: joined to a horse, part of him is human, but nothing remains of his former part. Because a distinction must be maintained in the high citadel of the sky, they differ in nature: whether they are two separate figures <note>Like the Twins (Gemini) or the Fishes (Pisces).</note> or a single figure of double form. 

Even Erigone <term>Erigone: a poetic name for Virgo, often identified with Astraea or Justice.</term> is counted among the double stars. The reason for this appearance is twofold: for summer ends and autumn begins while both meet in the middle of the Virgin. Therefore, the tropical signs <term>Tropical: From the Greek *tropos* (turn); these signs—Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn—mark the "turning points" of the sun at the equinoxes and solstices.</term> proceed before all others in pairs, like the Wool-bearer <note>Aries the Ram.</note>, the Claws <note>original: *chelis*; referring to Libra, which was often depicted as the Claws of the Scorpion.</note>, the Crab, and the Goat. These retain double powers within a connected body. 

Consider also the brother stars <note>Gemini.</note> who follow the Dog-star: one supplies the flowering times of spring, while the other carries forward the thirsty summer. Both are wet with dew, yet each also feels the heat. One represents the strength of the fading season, and the other the strength of the approaching one. One part belongs to the first age, while the other lot belongs to the second part. 

Likewise the Archer, who precedes you, Capricorn; he is said to be carried along formed in a double image. The milder part of autumn claims his soft limbs, and he carries the form of a man while the animal limbs govern his back.

<summary>Manilius explains the "double" nature of certain zodiac signs, such as Gemini, Virgo, and Sagittarius, noting how their dual forms represent the transition between seasons.</summary>
<keywords>zodiac, double signs, Sagittarius, Virgo, Gemini, seasons, Erigone, tropical signs, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of the zodiac signs, moving from their physical "double" nature into how they govern the transitions of the seasons and their orientation in the sky. It specifically addresses how certain signs mark the boundary between winter and spring, and introduces the concept of diurnal (daytime) and nocturnal (nighttime) signs.</meta>

<page-num>23 V</page-num>
<page-type>text</page-type>

Now already the signs receive winter in its seasons,
And those two seasons of the Fishes which Aries sends before itself;
The two lead the winter, they close it, and the deep spring begins
When the sun, returning, runs down through the watery stars.
Winter showers meet with those of spring,
Beyond which it has floating signs above the face <note>original: *oris*; likely referring to the head or "face" of the constellation Pisces as it transitions into Aries.</note>.

->Which signs are different<-

->How three signs joined together oppose the nine signs<-

And as if a sedition <note>original: *seditio*; used here to describe a "revolt" or "opposition" in the way the constellations are oriented compared to the others.</note> holds the sky, behold the Bull,
With his haunches <note>original: *clumbus*, likely a scribal error for *lumbus* (loins or flank).</note>, and the Twins, and the Crab with its shell-like feet;
While the rest arise with upright limbs,
Do not wonder at the delays when the sun, through adverse stars,
Lifts up the spring year during the slow months.

->Which signs are nocturnal and which are diurnal<-

Nor let the nocturnal and diurnal signs escape you,
Which are clearly distinguished by their own law.
It is not by darkness or light that they complete their night,
For the name would then be common to all with no distinction,
Since all of them shine in turns throughout the seasons,
And now days follow those, now nights follow these.
But to those for whom that parent of the world <term>Nature: personified here as the creator of the cosmic order.</term> has been consecrated,
She assigned parts of time by a fixed position.
For the sign of the Archer and the swift Lion,
And the one looking back at his own back with the golden fleece <note>The Ram (Aries), often depicted looking backward toward the following signs.</note>,
Now he sings of the Fishes, the Scorpion, and the Crab’s blow <note>original: *ictu*; likely referring to the "strike" or "pinch" of the Crab or the sting of the Scorpion.</note>.

<summary>This page describes the transition from winter to spring via Pisces and Aries, notes the unusual backward rising of Taurus, Gemini, and Cancer, and introduces the astrological classification of signs as Diurnal or Nocturnal based on their inherent nature rather than visibility.</summary>
<keywords>Zodiac, Seasons, Pisces, Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Diurnal signs, Nocturnal signs, Astronomy, Avienus</keywords>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the zodiac signs are classified, specifically focusing on the distinction between diurnal (daytime) and nocturnal (nighttime) signs, and their associations with land or water.</meta>

<page-num>24r</page-num>

Whether neighboring in their location or divided into equal parts,
All are said to be situated under a diurnal <term>Diurnal: relating to the day; in astrology, these signs are traditionally more active or "extraverted."</term> lot.
The rest are neither joined by number nor by change of position,
Nor do they bear as many signs placed between them in their locations.
Some have called six signs common and diurnal:
Castor <note>The Gemini twins, Castor and Pollux.</note> and the Sheep <note>Aries.</note> which are from the first sign,
The Wool-bearer <note>Aries again, or perhaps the group starting from Aries.</note>; while six starting from the Scales <note>Libra.</note> are seen to be nocturnal.
There are also those to whom diurnal signs are pleasing because they rise as masculine,
While the feminine signs rejoice in the night and its safe shadows.
And there are some things that speak for themselves without any teacher:
The Crab <note>Cancer.</note> among the rocky waves and the Fishes rejoicing in the poured-out sea
Owe their race to Neptune <term>Neptune: the Roman god of the sea; here representing the watery nature of certain signs.</term>.
And just as stars are judged to be of a terrestrial lot:
The Bull <note>Taurus.</note> is the leader of the herd and proud in his kingdom;
Aries is placed as the leader of the woolly flock;
The predatory Lion <note>Leo.</note> and the Scorpion with its thorny weapons.
There are also signs of a middle law, common to both:
Capricorn is ambiguous on the land, while Aquarius is among the waves,
And a portion of the Water-bearer <note>Aquarius.</note> shines as marine;
His life is lived mixed, the humid joined with the terrestrial.

->Which signs are fertile<-

One must not turn the mind away from even the smallest causes,
Nor was anything in the world created in need or in vain.
The race of the Crab is fertile, as is the sharp Scorpio,
And the Fishes, filling the seas with their offspring.

<summary>This page outlines the astrological classification of zodiac signs into diurnal and nocturnal groups, as well as their elemental associations with land (terrestrial) and water (aquatic), concluding with the signs considered most fertile.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the classification of the zodiac signs, moving from their elemental natures (land/water) and timing (diurnal/nocturnal) to their physical postures and perceived physical "defects." This text draws heavily from Manilius’s *Astronomica*, a first-century Latin poem on astrology.</meta>

<page-num>24v</page-num>

The Virgin <note>Virgo</note> is sterile, joined in nature to the similar Lion <note>Leo</note>.
The Water-bearer <note>Aquarius</note> pours forth his head or those births he has captured.
Capricorn remains in both states <note>Referring to its dual nature as both a land and water creature.</note> with its mixed body.
And so too does the Centaur <note>Sagittarius</note> who shines with his bow drawn tight.
The Ram <note>Aries</note> is common <note>original: *communis*; meaning shared or intermediate in properties</note>, as are the Scales <note>Libra</note> which balance the seasons,
And I count the Twins <note>Gemini</note> and the Bull <note>Taurus</note> in an equal category.

### Concerning the running signs, then the upright, then the sitting and lying signs.

Do not think these are mere fictions; the nature of things has dictated that some signs are "running."
Such as the Lion, the Bow-bearer <note>Sagittarius</note>, and the Ram twisted into its horns.
Or those that are balanced upright, standing on their own limbs,
Like the Virgin, the Twins, and the Water-bearer pouring out his waves.
Then there are those that sit as if weary, reflecting sluggish minds:
The Bull, drowsy once the plow is removed from its neck;
The Scales, ascending to their labor in the completed circle <note>original: *emerito orbe*; referring to the sun's path at the equinox</note>;
And you, Capricorn, contracted by your own frost among the stars.
In contrast, the Crab <note>Cancer</note> lies stretched out upon its wide belly,
The Scorpion rests with its flat body upon the earth,
And the Fishes <note>Pisces</note> are always lying on their slanted sides.

## Concerning the impaired signs

If you examine all these things with skillful care,
You will find stars cheated of their missing limbs <term>Impaired signs: In ancient astrology, certain constellations were described as "maimed" or "defective" because they were depicted as only partial figures or missing body parts.</term>.
The Scorpion consumes its arms <note>In ancient astronomy, the "claws" of the Scorpion originally extended to form the constellation we now know as Libra.</note> within the Scales; the Bull
Sinks down, lame with a bent foot; the eyes of the Crab
Are missing; for the Centaur, one eye remains and he searches for the other.

--- Page 59 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical description of the zodiacal signs, moving from their physical postures to their seasonal associations and the geometric relationships (specifically "trines") that govern astrological fate. The text continues to draw from Manilius’s *Astronomica*, particularly Book 2, which deals with the relationships between signs.</meta>

<page-num>251</page-num>

Thus the world consoles our misfortunes in the stars
And teaches us by example to endure losses patiently,
Since every order of fortune depends upon the heavens,
And even the constellations themselves are formed with weak limbs <note>original: *debilibus membris*. Manilius suggests that because some constellations are depicted as partial or "maimed," humans should accept their own physical imperfections as part of the divine order.</note>.
The signs are powerful in their own proper seasons:
Summer rises from the Twins <note>Gemini</note>, Autumn from the Virgin <note>Virgo</note>;
Winter <note>original: *Bruma*; the winter solstice</note> begins with the Archer <note>Sagittarius</note> or the Fishes <note>Pisces</note>.
The stars are assigned to the four quarters of the earth;
The winter signs oppose the summer ones, and the spring oppose the autumn.
Nor is it enough to know the specific shapes of the signs,
But also the agreement by which the fates move, and the treaty in which the universe rejoices,
And how some signs follow others by chance and by position.
As the circle of the signs is closed in its rightful orbit,
A line runs out into three equal lengths <note>This refers to the "Trine" aspect, where a triangle is drawn within the zodiacal circle to connect signs of the same element.</note>
And joins itself in turn at its outermost ends.
And whichever signs <insert>are given</insert> as trigonal <note>triangular</note>,
Within them the angle of the star falls three times.
These signs remain divided, distant across the lands:
The Wool-bearer <note>Aries</note> and, at equal intervals, the two signs of the Lion <note>Leo</note>
And the Archer <note>Sagittarius</note> look upon each other from their divided origins <note>Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius form the Fire Trine.</note>.
The stars of the Virgin <note>Virgo</note> and the Bull <note>Taurus</note> harmonize with Capricorn <note>Virgo, Taurus, and Capricorn form the Earth Trine.</note>.
The rest of the triangular signs follow a similar reasoning;
They are present for the whole world because they were established for the whole,
Whether the form be different on the right or on the left.
Those which follow and those which precede are said to be "on the right."

<keywords>astrology, Manilius, Zodiac, Trines, seasons, Aries, Leo, Sagittarius, Virgo, Taurus, Capricorn, geometry</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological aspects from the previous page (25r). While the previous page introduced the "Trine" (triangular) relationship between signs of the same element, this page concludes that thought and introduces the "Square" (quadrate) aspect, where signs are separated by 90 degrees. The author, the Roman poet Manilius, uses geometric metaphors—like the carpenter’s square—to explain how these celestial harmonies are constructed.</meta>

<page-num>25v</page-num>

Capricorn will be to the right of the Bull <note>Taurus</note>, and the Virgin <note>Virgo</note> to the left.
This is enough of an example: once observed, the universe is built 
by four parts <note>The "Square" or "Quartile" aspect</note> joined by sides of equal length, 
whose positions a perpendicular rod <note>original: *normalis uirgula*; a reference to a carpenter’s square used to create right angles</note> designates. 
These corners are held by the Balance <note>Libra</note> and the Horned Goat <note>Capricorn</note>, 
who looks upon the other; then the Ram <note>Aries</note> and, at equal intervals, 
the Crab <note>Cancer</note>; but here, to the left, are the stars of the rising Balance. 
For the preceding signs are always considered to be on the right. 
Indeed, run through all the parts in their entirety, 
and imagine the Earth framed by the twelve signs in a square; 
their powers are returned in order according to this proposed plan.

### On the measurement of triangles and squares in degrees <note>original: *p'tes* (partes). In ancient astronomy, "parts" usually refers to the 360 degrees of the zodiacal circle.</note>

But if anyone is satisfied with merely counting the squares, 
and thinks the world is divided by four signs, 
or if anyone wishes to adorn a triangle with three signs, 
so that he may demand allied powers and friendly risings, 
and find the kindred treaties of the world through the stars, 
he will be mistaken. For although there are five signs on every side, 
when those stars which are carried in every fifth place 
are born to perceive the trine <term>trigoni</term> <note>A "trine" is an astrological aspect where two signs are 120 degrees apart, forming a triangle within the circle.</note>, 
they will not have the power, but will only possess the name; 
they have lost the gifts of their position, which nowhere resists. 
For since there are three hundred and sixty degrees <note>original: *ter uicene* (thrice twenty added to three hundred)</note> of the circle through the signs, 
which the heat of the Sun <note>original: *phoebi*; Phoebus Apollo, representing the sun</note> circles, 
a third part of that number indeed makes up a single side.

<summary>This page explains the geometric "Square" aspect in astrology, identifying the cardinal signs (Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn) as its points, and clarifies that a true "Trine" aspect is based on a measurement of 120 degrees rather than simply counting signs.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, geometry, Square aspect, Trine, zodiac, Manilius, degrees, cardinal signs, Sun's path</keywords>

--- Page 61 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating astrological "aspects"—the geometric angles between stars. Having established the shapes (triangles and squares) on the previous page, the author now warns the reader that simply counting the number of zodiac signs is insufficient; one must measure the precise degrees to ensure the geometry is perfect.</meta>

<page-num>26r</page-num>
<page-type>text</page-type>

Produced into three parts through the signs of a triangle <note>The "Trine" aspect, where planets are 120 degrees apart.</note>,
Yet the final line does not return this total number
If one marks sign by sign and not degree <note>original: *pars* (part); the standard term for a degree in ancient astronomy.</note> by degree.
And although two stars are situated in these signs,
If you wish to compare the very first degree of the prior sign
To the very last degree of the next, you will never note the correct sum;
They will fulfill 150 <note>original: *ter quinquagenas*; literally "thrice fifty."</note> degrees in their order.
The number will overstep the shape and the boundaries of the following sign;
Therefore, although the signs <gloss>signs</gloss> are called triangular,
When they are divided <gloss>divided</gloss> by degrees <gloss>degrees</gloss>, they do not preserve those triangles.
This same hope fails regarding the square signs,
Even though the number that constructs the entire circuit
Returns a square through 90 <note>original: *ter triginta*; "thrice thirty."</note> degrees across the stars.
It happens that from the first part of a preceding sign,
If the measuring rod of the following sign leads to the final part,
The sum of the first will result in 120 <note>original: *bis sexagenas*; "twice sixty."</note> degrees.
And if the first part of two joined signs is compared
To the middle number that passes through the full signs,
It doubles the thirty degrees, but the third part will be missing.
And whether one computes the fourth star from the fourth sign,
The degrees of a single sign will suffer shipwreck <note>original: *naufragium*; a poetic metaphor for a mathematical calculation "sinking" or failing.</note> within them.
Therefore, it is not enough to have counted triangles by signs,
Nor the reliability of the square heavens by groups of four signs.
If by chance you wish to fashion the shape of a square,
You must do so either with three equal members or with triangles.

<meta>Flourished mark beneath the final verse.</meta>

<summary>The author explains the necessity of calculating astrological aspects by individual degrees (360 in a circle) rather than by whole zodiac signs. He demonstrates how counting only by signs leads to mathematical "shipwreck," resulting in totals like 150 or 120 degrees instead of the required 120 (for trines) or 90 (for squares).</summary>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological aspects, specifically focusing on the geometric precision required to define "Trines" (triangles) and "Squares." It transitions from the critique of counting signs to explaining the differing powers of these geometric relationships and introduces the "Sextile" (hexagon) aspect.</meta>

<page-num>26v</page-num>

Here it demands as much as the sum of one hundred;
There it loses a tenth, and thus the order is reconciled <note>original: *sic conuenit ordo*; this refers to the calculation of a 90-degree "Square" aspect (100 minus 10).</note>.
And whatever angle favors all four quarters throughout,
Or wherever a line marks out locations in a triple course,
It will correctly leave behind the calculated losses
In turn, sharing affections and mutual rights of favor.
Therefore, not every birth chart <term>genitura: a horoscope or individual's astrological map</term> possesses trines <note>Trines are "aspects" where planets are 120 degrees apart, forming a triangle.</note>
That are in agreement simply by their signs; nor, when signs happen to be square,
Do they immediately maintain a commercial exchange <note>original: *comertia reru(m)*; a metaphor for the way planets "communicate" or influence one another across the sky.</note> of affairs among themselves.
For it makes a difference whether the line exhausts the view of the specific degrees <note>original: *partis*; literally "parts," the standard term for degrees in ancient astronomy.</note>,
And whether, once the measure of the number which the circle encompasses is withdrawn,
Three lines or four lines drawn from every side complete the shape—
Lines which reason commands to proceed across many signs.
Sometimes, among those things mentioned by numbers throughout the circle,
The power of the signs in a triangle is far greater
Than those which bear the title of square under every fourth sign.
The line of the former is higher, being drawn from a remote temple <note>original: *sumoto templo*; Manilius uses "temple" to refer to a specific region or station of the sky.</note>;
That line travels closer to the heavens and recedes from the earth,
Whereas the vision of the squares approaches the lands more closely,
And sends its influence down through the tainted air to our breezes.

## On hexagonal signs

<margin>There are assemblies for the signs</margin>
There are also signs placed in the tenth position by alternating turns <gloss>through</gloss>
They maintain mutual treaties, though not with great consensus,
Because the line is bent reluctantly in a narrow circle;
For when individual boundaries are formed through such lines...

<summary>Manilius explains that the geometric power of a "Trine" (triangle) is spiritually superior and "higher" than a "Square," as the latter is closer to the earthly, tainted atmosphere. He also begins to define the "Sextile" aspect, which he views as a weaker, more constricted connection between signs.</summary>

<keywords>astrological aspects, Trine, Square, Sextile, degrees, geometry of the heavens, celestial influence, birth charts</keywords>

--- Page 63 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of geometric aspects in the zodiac. Having previously discussed the "Trine" (triangle) and "Square" (quadrate) aspects, Manilius now introduces the "Sextile" aspect, which forms a hexagon by connecting every other sign.</meta>

<page-num>27r</page-num>

The angle divides the stars by every alternating sign,
And the line is curved in a circle through six windings <note>This describes the "Sextile" aspect, where lines connect every second sign to form a hexagon within the zodiacal circle.</note>.
From Taurus it comes to Cancer; then, having touched Virgo,
It enters Scorpio; from there it reaches you, stiff Capricorn,
And the Twins <note>original: *piscis*; Manilius refers to the two fish of Pisces here, not Gemini.</note> from you; and touching the stars
Opposite to Taurus, it finishes the circle where it began.
The path for another drawing lies through the signs passed over <note>The first set connected the "even" signs (2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12); this second path connects the "odd" signs (1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11).</note>;
And though I pass over those things which were individually said by me,
A similar circle is made with just as many turns.
Therefore, they flee from views looking across <note>This refers to signs that are "inconjunct" or "averse," meaning they do not form a major geometric aspect and therefore cannot "see" or influence one another.</note>,
<margin>which are seen</margin>
Inwardly they incline from the extremes;
On both sides they lie hidden from a straight gaze.
Every third sign is hidden in a turned-back retreat,
And the line which follows in the vaulted sky,
Which crosses individual stars only in its circuit,
Is either far off or wanders in another part of heaven,
And sends weak powers from a distance into the circle.
If, however, Phoebus <note>The Sun.</note> is near to them under the law,
And the gender is not divided among the passing stars:
Masculine signs respond to masculine; the other six
Of the feminine world join together in their dealings <term>comertia: literally "commerce" or "exchange," used here to describe the astrological affinity between signs of the same gender</term>.
For if anything obeys other figures,
Kindred stars lie under fixed laws.
<margin>gender</margin>
<insert>On joined signs and when they are in the sixth place.</insert>
Now indeed, no power is added to signs that are clinging together <note>original: *hærentibus... signis*; signs that are immediately adjacent to one another (like Aries and Taurus) were thought to have no relationship because they lack a geometric aspect.</note>.

<summary>Manilius explains the "Sextile" aspect, a hexagonal relationship connecting every other sign, and introduces the classification of signs by gender (masculine and feminine).</summary>
<keywords>Sextile aspect, Hexagon, Zodiacal geometry, Sign gender, Masculine signs, Feminine signs, Inconjunct signs, Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, Pisces</keywords>

--- Page 64 ---
<meta>This page continues Manilius's exploration of zodiacal geometry. After discussing the "Sextile" aspect and signs that are "averse" (unable to "see" one another due to their positions), he now transitions into the "Opposition" aspect—signs that face each other directly across the celestial circle.</meta>

<page-num>27v</page-num>

For the favor of agreement is taken away from those whose sight is removed;
They carry all things within themselves, being the only ones who can hold their own minds.
There are also signs turned away, connected by five places throughout the circle <note>This refers to the "quincunx" or "inconjunct" relationship, where signs are five signs apart (150 degrees) and traditionally share no commonality in element or modality.</note>,
Where **masculine** signs are always besieged by **feminine** ones in turn.
Some harmony is granted to these unequal signs,
And the sixth signs are changed so that they provide no helpful strength.
The line is not drawn through the entire circle,
But it creates two signs removed from the middle four;
The third drawing, once the circle is assumed, does not suffice to complete the path.

## Regarding Opposing Signs

And those stars shine forth from divided parts of the sky,
Which hang with an opposing face across the middle of the world,
And remain contrary to one another, divided by the whole of heaven.
Although they are carried along, removed by the **seventh place** <note>In ancient astrology, the "seventh place" is the sign directly opposite the first (counting 180 degrees across the wheel).</note>,
They nevertheless maintain their powers from that great distance,
Administering their influence in either war or peace, as the times demand—
Now for the Sun <note>original: *phoebo*</note>, now for the stars, and now for distant shores.
If by chance you wish to commemorate the signs that are opposites
By their own titles and their proper seats, remember:
Oppose **Capricorn**, the midwinter solstice, to **Cancer**;
Oppose the **Wool-bearer** <note>Aries</note> to **Libra**, for in both, the night and day are equal;
Oppose **Virgo** <note>original: *erigone*</note> to **Pisces**, and the **Youth of the Urn** <note>Aquarius</note> to the **Lion**;
When **Scorpio** shines at the very top, **Taurus** is at the very bottom;
And the **Bow-bearer** <note>Sagittarius</note> sets while the **Twins** are rising in the circle;
They observe their opposing courses among themselves.

<vocab>Manilius, Astronomica, astrology, zodiacal signs, Capricorn, Cancer, Libra, Pisces, Leo, Scorpio, Taurus, Latin verse</vocab>

<summary>This page describes zodiacal signs that lack geometric "sight" of one another before transitioning into a detailed list of the six pairs of opposing signs that face each other directly across the sky.</summary>

<keywords>astrology, opposition aspect, zodiac pairs, celestial geometry, Manilius, solstice, equinox</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of opposing signs (those 180 degrees apart). Manilius explains that while opposition usually implies conflict due to contrasting seasons, certain pairs find harmony through shared gender or the balance of day and night during the equinoxes.</meta>

<page-num>28r</page-num>

But since they shine with contrary signs in opposition,
Nature nevertheless allows them to be joined at times;
And since mutual harmony arises from these examples,
Masculine signs are joined to their equals, and if divided <note>original: *divisa*; referring to the division of the zodiac into masculine and feminine signs.</note>, they follow their own kind.
It corresponds, for instance, that the Fishes <note>Pisces</note> and the star of the Virgin <note>Virgo</note>
Fly in opposition, yet they love their common laws;
For while their nature conquers their position, the signs themselves are conquered.
By the seasons, **the Crab** <note>original: *Caneri* (Cancer)</note> resists you, O **Capricorn**;
Feminine to feminine, just as summer disagrees with midwinter.
From the one comes stiffness, ice, and fields whitening with snow;
From the other comes thirst, sweat, and a world stripped bare upon the hills.
The summer days are equaled by the cold night of the winter solstice;
Thus nature wages war and sets the year at variance.
Do not wonder that stars fight for my part <note>The poet is explaining the "hostility" between signs at opposite points of the seasonal cycle.</note>.
But the sign of the Wool-bearer <note>Aries</note> and the Scales <note>Libra</note> do not fight;
Although the spring differs entirely from the time of autumn,
Filling the one with ripe fruits and the other with flowers,
Yet by equal reasoning, the night is equal to the days;
They bring about the seasons with a similar fabric of harmony,
With days mixed at the midpoint between winter and summer,
Truly observing both at the turning points <term>articuli|the equinoxes, viewed as the "joints" or hinges of the year</term> of time,
Lest the stars should turn away from one another in a hostile sky.
Such will be the logic applied to the divided signs.

**Under the protection of which god each sign falls**

**Having observed these things, the next concern is as follows:**

<summary>Manilius explains that while opposing signs like Cancer and Capricorn represent seasonal extremes (summer vs. winter), others like Aries and Libra find harmony through the balance of the equinoxes. This leads into the "tutelage" of the gods over the zodiac.</summary>

<keywords>opposition, Pisces, Virgo, Cancer, Capricorn, Aries, Libra, equinox, solstice, seasons, elements, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 66 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of celestial relationships from the previous page, moving from the geometric harmonies of the signs to the specific divine guardians assigned to them and the beginning of "melothesia"—the mapping of the human body onto the zodiac.</meta>

<page-num>28v</page-num>

Learn now of the guardianships and the divine powers assigned to the signs, and what nature has dedicated to each god of the world, when she gave divine countenances to great virtues and hid various powers within her sacred divinity, so that a personage <note>original: *persona*; referring to the "mask" or character of a deity</note> might add weight to these matters.

Pallas <note>Minerva/Athena</note> protects the **Wool-bearer** <note>Aries</note>; Cytherea <note>Venus, named for the island of Cythera</note> guards the **Bull** <note>Taurus</note>.
Phoebus <note>Apollo</note> guards the beautiful **Twins** <note>Gemini</note>; the Cyllenian <note>Mercury, born on Mount Cyllene</note> the **Crab** <note>Cancer</note>.
Jupiter himself, along with the Mother of the Gods <note>Cybele</note>, rules the **Lion** <note>Leo</note>.
The **Virgin** <note>Virgo</note> belongs to ear-bearing Ceres <note>goddess of agriculture</note>; and the **Scales** <note>Libra</note> were forged by Vulcan.
The fighting **Scorpion** <note>Scorpio</note> clings to Mavors <note>Mars</note>.
Diana protects the **Hunting Man** <note>Sagittarius</note>, even in his equine parts;
And Vesta warms the narrow stars of **Capricorn**.
Opposite the star of Jupiter <note>Leo</note> is Juno’s **Aquarius**;
And Neptune recognizes his own **Fishes** <note>Pisces</note> in the sea.

From here, great moments of your future will come to you, when your reason takes the stars and constellations as its care. You shall seek arguments and paths from every part, so that through your intellect, the power divided among the gods may rise, and mortal hearts may fulfill the faith of heaven.

**Which human limbs are assigned to each sign.**

Receive now the parts of man divided among the stars, and how individual signs appear in their own figures, within which they exercise the chief powers of the entire body. The **Ram** <note>Aries</note> is the head, the prince before all else; <insert>the Bull</insert> claims the neck as the most beautiful portion of its rank and lot...

<summary>This page lists the tutelary Roman deities who guard each zodiac sign and introduces the concept of zodiacal anatomy, where each sign rules a specific part of the human body starting with Aries and the head.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, Marcus Manilius, zodiac guardians, Pallas, Venus, Apollo, Mercury, Jupiter, Ceres, Vulcan, Mars, Diana, Vesta, Juno, Neptune, melothesia, zodiacal anatomy</keywords>

--- Page 67 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "melothesia"—the mapping of the zodiac signs to the human body—and begins a discussion on the "aspects" or relationships between signs, described here as sensory interactions like seeing and hearing.</meta>

<page-num>29r</page-num>

[The neck belongs to] the **Bull** <note>original: *Taurus*</note>, and in the **Twins** <note>original: *Gemini*</note> the arms are written as connected to the shoulders with equal strength; the chest is located under the **Crab** <note>original: *Cancer*</note>. The region of the sides and the shoulder-blades belong to the **Lion** <note>original: *Leo* Gemini</note>; those parts descend to the **Virgin** <note>original: *Virgo*</note> as her own lot. The **Scales** <note>original: *Libra*</note> rule the buttocks, and the **Scorpion** <note>original: *Scorpius*</note> delights in the groin. The thighs fall to the **Centaur** <note>Sagittarius</note>. **Capricorn** commands both knees. The shanks belong to the **Water-pourer** <note>Aquarius; the OCR "Arborium" is likely a transcription error for *robur*, meaning strength or trunk</note>, and the **Fishes** <note>original: *Pisces*</note> claim the rights of the feet.

**The relations of the signs: hearing, sight, love, and hatred.**

The stars, bound to one another by their own laws, come together; thus they carry out the commerce of things. In turn, they offer sight to one another and cling to each other's ears, or they harbor hatred and treaties. Certain signs, once joined, <margin>but with fury</margin> are led at once by full favor. For this reason, grace sometimes exists between opposing signs, and those that are allied wage war. In a foreign seat, they double their union through all time, and both fight and flee by turns with strength.

But when God established the universe into all its laws, He also divided the affections among the varying stars. He brought the eyes of some to others, and the ears of others to others. He joined the friendships of these under a certain treaty, so that they might be able to see and hear one another, love some, and stir up harm and war. In this way, there would be indulgence according to the nature of their own lot.

<summary>This page maps the twelve zodiac signs to specific parts of the human body and introduces the concept of astrological aspects, framing the relationships between signs as social interactions like friendship, sight, and hearing.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "aspects" or sensory relationships between the signs of the zodiac, describing how they "see" and "hear" one another across the celestial sphere. ...so that they might love one another and always please each other.
Just as we see many natures among men,
who take their origin from the signs that form their birth,
the **Ram** <note>original: *Aries*</note> keeps his own counsel, as is worthy of a leader.
But he hears the **Scales** <note>original: *Libra*</note>; thereafter, the **Bull** <note>original: *Taurus*</note> frustrates
him with love, and weaves deceit for the **Wool-bearer** <note>original: *lanigero*; the Ram</note>, and beyond
he sees and hears the shining **Fishes** <note>original: *Pisces*</note> through the stars.
The mind [of the Bull] is captured by the **Virgin** <note>original: *Virgine*</note>; just as he once carried
**Europa** <note>Referring to the myth where Jupiter, disguised as a bull, carried the princess Europa across the sea</note> on his back, holding his horns with her left hand,
and having put on the guise of Jove, he is led to the ears of the **Twins** <note>original: *Gemini*</note>.
Toward the youth <note>Aquarius</note> pouring eternal waters for the **Fishes**,
and toward the **Fishes** themselves he turns his mind, and his eyes toward the **Lion** <note>original: *Leo*</note>.
The **Crab** <note>original: *Cancer*</note> and **Capricorn** are set in the stars opposite one another.
[The Crab] turns its mind toward itself and stretches with its eyes into silent things,
and the ear of the **Crab** is fitted to the star of the **Water-bearer** <note>original: *Aquarius*</note>.
But the **Lion** also joins his mind and ear with the **Twins**.
The **Centaur** <note>original: *Centaurus*; the Archer or Sagittarius</note> loves the twin stars of **Capricorn**.
From his own region he watches the **Bull**, but he hears the **Scorpion** <note>original: *Scorpion*</note>
and attempts to weave deceit for the **Archer** <note>original: *Sagittifero*</note>.
The **Scales** follow their own senses, and only by seeing
the **Wool-bearer** <note>Aries</note>; while the **Scorpion** is embraced by the spirit below.
That sign [the Scorpion] sees the **Fishes** and hears the **Scales** through all things.
Likewise, the **Bow-holder** <note>original: *Arcitenens*; Sagittarius</note> is accustomed to obey the great **Lion**
with both ears and eyes, and he watches the sign of the **Water-pourer** <note>original: *Aquarius*</note>
alone out of all the stars.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological relationships from page 30r. The golden stars yielded to the **Fleece-bearer** <note>original: *lanigero*; the constellation Aries</note>;
The **Centaur** <note>original: *centaurus*; Sagittarius</note> himself yields his back to its portion.
To such an extent is man conquered by the gift of those stars,
That even the **trine** <note>a "trine" or *trigonum* is a group of three signs spaced 120 degrees apart, usually sharing an elemental nature</note> of the rising **Scales** <note>original: *libræ*; Libra</note> can be overcome.
Furthermore, there is a shorter reasoning to be followed concerning the signs:
For whatever stars shine fashioned in **human form**
Remain enemies to the stars of the **wild beasts**, and are conquered by them.
But nevertheless, each sign withdraws into its own private thoughts
And they carry secret weapons against their hidden enemies.
Those born under the **Fleece-bearer** <note>Aries</note> have war with those born under the **Virgin** <note>original: *uirgine*; Virgo</note>,
And with the **Scales** <note>Libra</note>, and the **Twin Fishes** <note>original: *gemini pisces*; Pisces</note> which the wave brought forth.
In the part of the **Bull** <note>original: *tauri*; Taurus</note>, those born under the **Crab** <note>original: *cancro*; Cancer</note> are brought
Into conflict; and against the **Claws** <note>original: *chelas*; the constellation Libra, formerly considered the "claws" of the Scorpion</note> and those which the sharp **Scorpion** <note>original: *scorpius*; Scorpius</note> provides,
And the **Fishes** and those whom the stars of the **Twins** <note>original: *geminorum*; Gemini</note> form.
With these, the **Fleece-bearer** and his trine are at war.
In the **Crab**, the seeds of **Capricorn** <note>original: *capricorni*</note> harm the **Twins**
And the offspring of the **Scales**, and those whom the star of the **Virgin** gives,
And those who are numbered under the opposing star of the **Bull**.
He shall be a companion to the **Fleece-bearer** and an enemy to the rabid **Lion** <note>original: *leonis*; Leo</note>;
But war is declared across all the stars
From the region of the **Bull**, who fears the **Twins** under the bow
Of the **Centaur**. The **Fishes** and you, frozen **Capricorn**,
A very great crowd attacks: the **Scales**, **Capricorn**, and opposite to him
The **Crab**, and the **Claws**; each relationship is a **square** <note>original: *quadratum*; an astrological aspect of 90 degrees, traditionally considered a point of tension or conflict</note>.
And those who are numbered among the signs of the **Fleece-bearer's** trine...

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological "warfare" (enmity between signs) from page 30v. While the previous page established the general rule that human-shaped signs are naturally at odds with animal-shaped signs, this page provides specific lists of which constellations "flee" or "oppose" one another based on their positions in the zodiac and their geometric relationships (aspects).</meta>

The **Scorpion** <note>original: *Scorpius*</note> is believed to be a fertile enemy to just as many;
He flees the **youth of the sea** <note>original: *Equoreum iuuenẽ*; the constellation Aquarius</note>, the **Twins** <note>original: *geminos*; Gemini</note>, the **Bull** <note>original: *taurũ*; Taurus</note>, and the **Lion** <note>original: *leonẽ*; Leo</note>.
He also avoids **Erigone** <note>a name for the constellation Virgo, after the mythological daughter of Icarius</note> and the **Scales** <note>original: *libramq*; Libra</note>, and is himself a figure of dread,
As are those who come forth from the constellation of the **Archer** <note>original: *sagittari*; Sagittarius</note>.
Those born under the **Twins**, the **Scales**, the **Virgin**, and the **Urn** <note>original: *urna*; another name for Aquarius, referring to his water jar</note>
Wish to suppress these by law, for nature commands it.
These same signs are said to be hostile to your offspring, **Capricorn**,
And to those whom the **Water-bearer** <note>original: *aquarius*</note> drenches with eternal waves.
Coming to the battle, he stirs up the entire **trine** <note>original: *trigonũ*; a grouping of three signs that form an equilateral triangle on the zodiacal wheel</note>,
A crowd living under the power of a single **wild beast** <note>referring to the animal-shaped signs</note>.
The neighboring **Water-bearer** presses upon the rising **Fishes** <note>original: *pisces*</note>,
As do the **Twin brothers** and those granted by the star of the **Virgin**,
And those born of the **Archer** who depart from the **Virgin**.
This is the only reason that provides weapons to those at birth,
And generates births into hatred and mutual desires [of conflict].
But for the most part, every third sign <note>this refers to the geometric aspect of signs; though modern astrology focuses on the 90-degree "square" as the source of tension, Manilius is mapping specific "transverse" gazes</note> remains hostile,
Fixed by law across the wheel with a malignant gaze,
And wherever opposite signs remain in their place,
And those which, being the **seventh** <note>original: *septima*; an "opposition" aspect, where signs are 180 degrees apart, looking directly at one another</note>, bear a direct gaze toward each other.
Every third sign is said to be the **trine** for both of them.
Hence it is no wonder if **the Sun** <note>original: *phoebus*</note> is not granted to the stars
Which are related to the opposite signs of the trine.
Through so many species of signs, contrary forces arise,
And bodies in as many ways as whenever hostile beings are created;
For it is no wonder that nature created nothing of conflict out of itself. <note>Manilius concludes that these celestial antipathies are the source of the natural conflicts and variety seen on Earth</note>

<summary>This page details specific astrological animosities, focusing on how Scorpio and Aquarius conflict with other signs. It explains that "warfare" between stars arises from their geometric positions, such as oppositions and trines, which dictates the character of those born under them.</summary>
<keywords>Scorpio, Aquarius, Zodiacal Aspects, Trine, Opposition, Erigone, Astrological Warfare, Manilius</keywords>

--- Page 72 ---
<meta>This page continues from the discussion on page 31r regarding the natural hostilities between different zodiac signs. However, the author here pauses his technical explanation of astronomical "warfare" to provide a philosophical reflection on the nature of human discord. He argues that the enmity seen in the stars is mirrored in the rarity of human friendship and the abundance of earthly crime, linking the macrocosm of the universe to the microcosm of human society.</meta>

<page-num>31v</page-num>

Nothing in the human heart is greater, nor ever rarer, than **friendship** <note>original: *amicitia*</note>.
There will be but one **Pylades** and one **Orestes** <note>A legendary pair of friends; Pylades helped Orestes avenge the murder of his father, Agamemnon, and they were famous for their willingness to die for one another</note> who would each prefer
To die himself; throughout the ages, a single instance of such death was considered a light burden.
One would snatch away the other's fate, while the other would not allow it.
And consider those two who were barely able to follow the path of punishment <note>An allusion to the story of Damon and Pythias</note>:
The **guarantor** <note>original: *sponso*; referring to Damon, who stood as a hostage for his friend</note> prayed that the accused could not return,
While the accused feared lest he release his surety <note>Pythias feared that by arriving late, his friend would have to be executed in his place</note>.
Therefore, through so many ages of men, so many eras and years,
Through so many wars and various labors even in times of peace,
Should **Fortune** seek for **loyalty**, she finds it scarcely anywhere.

But how great is the **mass of crimes** <note>original: *scelerum moles*</note> throughout all the centuries,
And what a burden of hatred exists, inexcusable upon the earth!
Fathers were led to their fates, and upon our very tombs
**Phoebus** <note>The sun god, Apollo</note> imposed darkness and fled the lands <note>A reference to the myth of Atreus and Thyestes; the sun was said to have turned back in the sky to avoid seeing a father unwittingly eat his own children at a banquet</note>.
Why should I speak of gutted cities and ruined temples,
The various disasters of peace, the mixing of poisons,
The treacheries of the forum, the slaughters within the city walls,
And the crowd that prowls under the very name of friendship?
Crime is performed in the open, and all things overflow with madness;
Right and wrong are entangled, and through the very **laws** themselves
Wickedness rages; now guilt triumphs over the punishments.
Truly, **discord** <note>original: *discordia*</note> conquers among the many.
Bodies are born as fragments scattered across the world,
And rare is the **treaty** <note>original: *foedus*</note> of faith or the tribute of peace;
Just as the heavens are divided, so the earth itself is at odds.

<summary>The author moves from astronomical technicalities to a moralizing reflection on the rarity of true friendship (citing Pylades and Orestes) and the prevalence of social discord and crime. This serves to justify why the zodiac signs are often in conflict, as the earth mirrors the divisions of the heavens.</summary>
<keywords>friendship, Pylades, Orestes, Phoebus, discord, human nature, law, macrocosm, microcosm</keywords>

<meta>This page continues from page 31v, where the author transitioned from discussing the scarcity of human friendship and the abundance of crime to the cosmic origins of these behaviors. <page-num>325</page-num>

And thus the races of men are carried by an unfriendly fate.
But if you also desire to recognize the **kindred signs** <note>Signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water) that naturally harmonize</note>
Which join souls together and are carried by a friendly fate:
Join the **Wool-bearer** <note>original: *Lanigeri*; referring to Aries the Ram</note> with the whole **triangle** <term>trigon: a group of three zodiac signs positioned 120 degrees apart, forming an equilateral triangle representing one of the four elements</term>.
Yet the **Ram** <note>Aries</note> is simpler and better than the **Lion** <note>Leo</note>;
It follows those born under itself and you, O **Centaur** <note>Sagittarius</note>,
More than it is revered: for it is a milder star.
<margin>is empty</margin> Nor do the minds of those [the Lion and Centaur] come together more into alliances <note>original: *foedera*; referring to the difficulty of these "beast" signs to form stable bonds compared to the gentler Ram</note>,
Exposed as they are to their own harm, and not by any frauds.
Nor does the gift of a soft nature consist in the body.
In those signs <note>The predatory signs of the Fire Trigon: Leo and Sagittarius</note> there is a wildness and a desire for prey,
And a **venal loyalty** <note>original: *venalisque fides*; suggesting that for these signs, loyalty is something that can be bought or sold</note> which forces one to exceed the true bounds of things
Through self-interest; nor is the gratitude for a deed long-lasting.
But when the members of the **Wool-bearer**'s triangle <note>The Fire Trigon</note>
Do not obey, they rarely wage wars over time,
Because the wildness of each forces them more throughout the ages.
Yet more strength must be counted in the **double-formed sign** <note>Sagittarius, who is half-man and half-horse</note>
In which a **man is mixed** <note>The human half of the Sagittarius constellation</note>, than under you alone, O **Nemean one** <note>Leo, the Lion of Nemea killed by Hercules</note>.
Therefore there is peace for these signs, yet mixed with complaints.
Five signs away, the **Bull** <note>Taurus</note> is joined to the star of **Capricorn**,
Nor do the minds of those come together more into alliances;
And the offspring of the **Virgin** <note>Virgo</note> and whoever else are created
Desire to be embraced by the **Bull**, but they often complain.
But those whom the **Claws** <note>original: *chelae*; the older name for the constellation Libra, which was once seen as the claws of Scorpio</note> and **Aquarius** bring forth at birth:
They possess a single heart and are joined by an **immobile faith** <note>original: *fideque immobile unitum*; emphasizing the exceptional stability of the Air Trigon (Libra, Aquarius, and Gemini)</note>.

<summary>This page details the astrological "trigons" (triplicities), explaining how signs within the Fire and Earth groups interact with varying degrees of harmony or friction. It highlights the gentleness of Aries compared to Leo and Sagittarius, the instability of Taurus and Capricorn, and the superior loyalty found in the Air signs like Libra and Aquarius.</summary>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological "trigons" or elemental triangles from page 32r. Having previously discussed the Fire signs (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius), the author now moves to the Water signs and the geometric relationships—squares and triangles—that determine whether individuals will be friends or enemies.</meta>

<page-num>32 v</page-num>

You will come to great success and find many friends.
**Scorpio** and the **Crab** <note>original: *cancrĩ*; referring to the sign of Cancer</note> are drawn together by brotherly names;
So too are those born of them at once, as well as those risen from the **Fishes** <note>Pisces</note>.
They harmonize, for their actions are often deceitful;
**Scorpio** sprinkles injuries under the name of a friend.
But for those in whose birth the **Fishes** are present,
These do not always remain of one heart;
They change their minds at times and break their treaties,
And they seek again hidden evils <note>original: *lues*; literally a plague or pestilence, used here to describe a toxic or deceptive character</note> that wander beneath the brow.
Thus, hatred and peace are to be noted from the signs.
Those born on earth are created in such light.
Yet it is not enough to rely on the signs alone;
Contemplate the place of the sky and the seat of the **wanderers** <term>wanderers: the planets (from the Greek *planētēs*), which move across the sky unlike the "fixed" stars of the zodiac</term>.
In their placement they vary their nature, and the line changes their powers.
For their own laws come to the **squares** <term>quadrate: an astrological aspect where signs are 90 degrees apart; ancient astrologers viewed this "square" relationship as a source of friction and "hatred"</term> and their own to the **triangles** <term>trigon: signs 120 degrees apart that share an element, creating natural "friendship"</term>.
And as the line runs through six tracts
<margin>the sky</margin>
Which cut through the middle, having crossed the world’s limit.
From here the world now gives strength, and now it takes it away:
Those things which take strength there, return it when moved here.
For it makes a difference whether a sign rises, or sinks, or falls.
Hatred is more frequent among those in opposing **squares**,
While bodies are judged by their signs and are friendly in **triangles**.
Nor is the reason obscure; for Nature also placed a fourth
Sign of the same kind; for through the circle
It marks the heaven with four equal divisions.

<summary>This page describes the deceptive nature of the Water signs (Scorpio, Cancer, and Pisces) and introduces the geometric foundations of astrology, where "triangles" create harmony and "squares" lead to discord.</summary>

--- Page 75 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 32v, moving from specific sign descriptions to the broader structure of the zodiac. Manilius explains how the signs are categorized by the seasons they inhabit, their physical forms (single or "double"), and the geometric relationships—squares and triangles—that dictate human bonds and fate.</meta>

<page-num>33r</page-num>

In these signs, God himself created the divisions of the year;
Spring is served by the **Ram** <note>original: *aries*</note>; the **Crab** <note>original: *cancrũ*; the sign of Cancer</note> provides the harvest of Ceres <note>the goddess of grain and agriculture, representing the heat of summer</note>, and the **Balance** <note>Libra</note> brings Bacchus <note>the god of wine and the vine, representing the autumn vintage</note>.
The **Goat** <note>Capricorn</note> and the **Balance** together bring the winter solstice <note>original: *brumam*</note>, and the **Fishes** <note>Pisces</note> are born to the cold.
Nor should one overlook those connected by a double figure,
Which hold their fourth place in the order: the two **Fishes**,
The **Twins** <note>Gemini</note> appearing as youths, the double form in the **Virgin** <note>Virgo; often depicted with dual attributes like wings or the scales of justice</note>,
And the two-natured **Centaur** <note>Sagittarius</note> whose bodies are joined as one. <gloss>to you</gloss>
So too the simple signs stand in a four-sided shape:
For the **Bull** <note>Taurus</note> has no companion, nor does any other join the thick-maned,
Dreadful **Lion** <note>Leo</note>, who has earned his place without another body;
So also the **Scorpion** and the **Water-Bearer** <note>Aquarius</note> are counted as single stars.
Thus, whichever signs remain established in the **square** <term>square: also called a "quadrate," an aspect where signs are 90 degrees apart. Ancient astrologers viewed this as a relationship of tension or seasonal balance</term> temple,
They reflect an equal share of duties or a similar strength of season.
And just as they remain related under such a treaty,
Therefore they mark their kin by these near degrees;
They agree with one another, and each image holds its children under its protection.
A line stretched out into a greater distance
Is that which, having passed through three signs, creates **triangles** <term>trigon: a relationship where signs are 120 degrees apart and share the same "element" (fire, earth, air, or water), considered the most harmonious aspect</term> among the stars.
These invite us toward friendships, the laws of the heart,
And degrees of blood; they lead through times clinging to the years.
And just as these distant lines meet after a long withdrawal,
So they join us together across greater intervals.
These are thought to be better, for they can join minds together
More firmly than the bond of blood, which sometimes fails;
Every third sign follows and supports its neighbors.

<meta>This page follows 33r, which established the basic geometry of the zodiac signs—distinguishing between "single" and "double" signs and their grouping into squares. Here, Manilius moves deeper into the internal structure of the signs, introducing the complex system of sub-divisions known as *Duodecatemoria*.</meta>

<page-num>33V</page-num>
<page-type>text</page-type>

The order of the stars shall be preserved for their hosts <note>original: *hospitibus*; referring to the signs as "houses" or hosts for the planets</note>,
As many as are moved by their own varying turning points <note>original: *cardinibus*; the four pivotal points of the sky: the rising, setting, noon, and midnight points</note>.
Those signs which the zodiac divided into the four parts of the world
Create a square, and they are judged as squares of no great length;
The number of signs is fewer than the uses of the turning points.
Add their own parts to the signs, and to those parts their own stars;
For nothing is bounded entirely, but they are carried along in a mixed state.
They give boundaries to the stars themselves and receive them in turn,
Which the logic of the heavens shall soon mark out for me, arranged in a fixed sub-order.
From all of these, the principle must be sought through the art of calculation,
So that you may learn the signs thoroughly once they have been peacefully inspected.

Behold now with keen sight a matter great in weight,
And marked everywhere by such a welcome name:
The **Twelve-Parts** <term>Duodecatemoria: From the Greek *duodeka* (twelve) and *morion* (part). This system divides each 30-degree zodiac sign into twelve smaller segments of 2.5 degrees each, allowing each sign to contain a miniature version of the entire zodiac.</term> marking out the causes into titles.
For since the constellations consist of thirty degrees each,
That whole number is again divided by twelve.
Therefore, the calculation teaches that there are two parts in equal shares,
And half-parts <note>i.e., 2.5 degrees</note> exist within these boundaries.
The **Twelve-Parts** consist of twelve units all together
In all the signs, which that Creator of the world
Assigned to just as many numbers with shining stars,
So that the stars might be allied by a law of alternation <gloss>perhaps</gloss> <note>original: *forte*; likely a scribal correction or alternative reading</note>,
And the world might be similar to itself, with all stars existing in all signs,
A harmony of whose mixtures might rule the body of the universe,
And there might be a mutual protection for a common cause.

<summary>Manilius introduces the concept of Duodecatemoria, a mathematical system where each 30-degree zodiac sign is subdivided into twelve 2.5-degree segments. This system demonstrates the interconnectedness of the cosmos, suggesting that every sign contains a micro-version of the entire zodiac.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, Duodecatemoria, celestial geometry, turning points, zodiac divisions, cosmic harmony, Manilius</keywords>

<page-num>341</page-num>

Therefore, because people are born under the same signs, 
They yet display different characters and conflicting desires; 
And often the logic <note>original: *ratio*; the underlying mathematical or cosmic principle</note> wanders into the cattle <note>Manilius refers to the "animal" signs of the zodiac, like Aries or Taurus, to explain how human traits might mirror animalistic ones</note>, and the male 
is followed by the female, and births are mixed <gloss>through</gloss> the stars <gloss>through</gloss> the offspring. 
As many stars as there are vary in their individual divided parts, 
under the changing laws of the **Twelve-Parts** <term>Duodecatemoria: The system where each 30° sign is divided into twelve 2.5° segments, each corresponding to one of the twelve signs of the zodiac.</term>.

Now I shall sing of what the nature of each is, and in what order it consists, 
Lest you wander aimlessly through unknown parts of the signs. 
The [sign] itself keeps the first parts in its own body <gloss>+</gloss> 
And the final logic is granted to the last stars <gloss>+</gloss>. 
Thus each sign keeps two [parts] for each star <gloss>through</gloss> each, 
and halves of its own part; the total is filled up <gloss>through</gloss> 
by thirty shares exactly in every sign. <note>Manilius is describing the 2.5 degree increments: 2 + 1/2 = 2.5. 2.5 degrees multiplied by 12 segments equals the full 30 degrees of a zodiac sign.</note>
This is one kind of logic, not always difficult; 
for nature has then placed things in many ways 
and drew out the paths and wished them to be sought through all things. 
Under the same name, another calculation is also arranged <gloss>through +</gloss>: 
In whatever part <gloss>degree</gloss> of a sign the Moon has stood at the time of birth, 
we must then arrange the calculation by these numbers <gloss>through</gloss>, 
as many as the stars that shine in the world follow in turn. 
Then, for that sign in which the Moon shone, 
remember to count those parts which are missing from this [full sign]; 
for every five [parts], the rule equally leads to thirty. <note>This describes a shortcut for calculating the Moon's sub-sign: since each 2.5° (five half-degrees) represents a full 30° sign in the sub-division, the poet is providing a mathematical ratio for the calculation.</note>
Here, when you fail <gloss>the value of omega</gloss> in numbers, then the remaining sum...
